L1397: ## Sofipironium gel
L1398: The AUC0-24 of the subjects receiving chronic topical administration of the anticipated therapeutic dose of sofipironium bromide gel, 15% (1 pump actuation applied once daily to each axilla) was used to predict the probability of AE occurrence using the final model. There was a positive relationship between sofipironium bromide exposure and TEAE related to blurred vision with higher rate of TEAE observed at highest exposure quartile. While a notably higher incidence of Treatment-Emergent Adverse Events (TEAE) associated with blurred vision was detected in the Q4 exposure quartile compared to the other three quartiles, clinical evidence suggests that the risk of individuals experiencing blurred vision with daily topical application of 15% Sofipironium Bromide Gel is considered to be low. In addition, most subjects treated with sofipironium bromide gel, 15% who experience blurred vision describe it as mild or moderate in severity and transient, meaning it is initially intermittent and then gradually resolves over time. Therefore, the positive relationship between the highest observed systemic exposure (AUC0-24) and blurred vision does not pose a significant safety risk when the gel is used at the intended daily dosage for topical administration.
L1399: ## Reviewer's Comments:
L1400: - *Given the substantial variability and erratic PK profiles of the topically applied product, the use of a noncompartmental analysis (NCA) without making any assumptions seems to be a reasonable approach for determining the exposure metric. This metric was utilized as input for the safety exposure-response (E-R) analysis.*
- *The Sponsor has chosen to exclusively perform safety exposure efficacy analyses rather than conducting an exposure efficacy investigation. This decision was based on the fact that the locally administered drug's mode of action primarily affects the targeted local area and is not directly correlated with systemic exposure. This approach is deemed acceptable from a regulatory standpoint.*
- *It's important to note that the AUC0-24 and Cmax used for the safety E-R analyses represent the maximum AUC0-24 and Cmax selected for a subject after multiple drug administrations throughout the entire treatment duration. These values may not have been estimated at the time when side effects were measured during treatment.*
- *While a positive relationship exists between the highest observed systemic exposure (AUC0-24) and blurred vision, there is no significant safety concern associated with the recommended daily topical administration dose of 15% sofipironium bromide gel, as supported by clinical evidence. (Refer to clinical review for further details).*

L1494: ## 1. Background
L1495: In this submission, the sponsor included reports of two animal carcinogenicity studies, one in rats and one in mice. These studies were intended to assess the carcinogenic potential of BBI-4000 when administered by subcutaneous injection in rats and by dermal in mice at appropriate drug levels for about 104 weeks. Results of this review have been discussed with the reviewing pharmacologist Dr. Li.
L1496: In this review, the phrase "dose response relationship" (trend) refers to the linear component of the effect of treatment, and not necessarily to a strictly increasing or decreasing mortality or tumor incidence rate as dose increases.
L1497: ## 2. Rat Study
L1498: In this study two separate experiments were conducted, one in male rats and one in female rats. In each of these two experiments there were three treated groups, and one vehicle control group. Two hundred eighty-eight Sprague-Dawley rats of each sex were assigned to three treated groups, and one vehicle control group by a stratified randomization scheme designed to achieve similar group mean body weights in equal size of 72 animals, as indicated in Table 1. The dose levels for the three treated groups were 0.3, 0.75, and 1.5 mg/kg/day for males and 0.5, 1.5, and 5 mg/kg/day for females, for up to 104 weeks. In this review, these dose groups were referred to as the low, medium, and high dose group, respectively. The vehicle control group received the vehicle (Sterile 0.9 % Sodium Chloride for Injection), administered by subcutaneous injection for about 104 weeks in the same manner as the treated groups.
L1499: **Table 1: Experimental Design in Rat Study**
L1500: Group Name	Group No.	Dose Level (mg/kg/day)	Number of Animal
Male	Female	Males	Females
Vehicle Control	1	0	0	72	72
Low	2	0.3	0.5	72	72
Medium	3	0.75	1.5	72	72
High	4	1.5	5	72	72
L1501: During the study period all animals were observed for general health/mortality and moribundity twice daily (near the start and end of each working day), abnormal findings were recorded throughout the study. Cage side observations were conducted for each carcinogenicity animal once daily during the dosing phase, except on days when detailed observations were conducted. Detailed observations were conducted for each carcinogenicity animal at least once prior to dosing on Day 1, and weekly thereafter throughout the dosing phase. Detailed physical examinations which included palpation masses were done weekly, the time of onset, location, size, appearance, and progression of each grossly visible or palpable mass, observed in carcinogenicity rats, was recorded weekly. Particular attention was paid to any superficial or palpable swellings, for which the location, size, consistency, time of first observation and subsequent history were recorded. Any animal showing signs of severe debility or intoxication, and if determined to be moribund or suffering excessively will be euthanized. Observations will include, but will not be limited to, evaluation for reaction to treatment. Histopathological examinations were performed on all animals found dead or killed moribund or sacrificed at the end of the experiment. Body weights were recorded one week before treatment commenced, on the day that treatment commenced (Week 0), at weekly intervals for the first 16 weeks of treatment, thereafter once every four weeks and before necropsy.
L1502: ## 2.1. Sponsor's analyses
L1503: ## 2.1.1. Survival analysis
L1504: In the sponsor's analysis, the tests for survival comparisons were performed for male and female rats separately with a two-sided risk for increasing and decreasing mortality with dose. Tests were performed for dose response (vehicle control and dosed groups only), and for each dosed group against vehicle control group, using a two-tailed log rank trend test (continuity-corrected, with 1 degree of freedom). The Kaplan-Meier's curves were presented graphically for male and female rats separately.
L1505: Where the test for trend was statistically significant, the highest dose group was excluded and the trend test repeated (using a one-tailed test in the direction identified with all groups included), until the test was no longer statistically significant.
L1506: Any animal with accidental injury that causes its death, or its unscheduled sacrifice was censored in the estimation. In addition, all animals still alive at the end of the experimental period were censored at the following day.
L1507: ## Sponsor's findings:
L1508: Sponsor's analysis showed the numbers of rats surviving to their terminal necropsy were 35 (48.6%), 38 (52.8%), 35 (48.6%), and 35 (48.6%) in the vehicle control, low, medium, and high dose groups, in male rats, respectively, and 20 (27.8%), 31 (43.1%), 18 (25%), and 30 (41.7%) in the vehicle control, low, medium, and high dose groups, in female rats, respectively. The sponsor's report showed no statistically significant differences in survival were observed across the dose groups. For both males and females, the trend test was not statistically significant when all groups for males or for females were included in the analysis (p=0.752 for males or p=0.322 for females). None of the pairwise comparisons were statistically significant.
L1509: ## 2.1.2. Tumor data analysis
L1510: In the sponsor's analysis, tests to compare tumor incidence were performed, with a one-sided risk for increasing incidence with dose. Tests were performed for dose response (vehicle control and dosed groups only) and for each dosed group against the vehicle control group. Log-rank methods were used to analyze the number of animals with tumors across treatment groups (Mantel 1966, Peto 1974, Peto et al 1980). Life table methods (PETO, R. et al 1980) will be used to compare tumor incidence over groups. The cut-off points for the interval-based test were 1 to 52, 53 to 78, 79 to 92, 93 to 104 weeks, and at terminal sacrifice. Actual dose levels were used as the scores. Tumors will be classified as "fatal" ("non-incidental") or "incidental" according as to whether they had a causal association with death or not, respectively. Fatal and non-fatal tumors were analyzed together, with separate stratum for each. For non-palpable tumors, each tumor was categorized as non-incidental if the tumor was a factor contributing towards the death of the animal, incidental otherwise. All animals that died after terminal sacrifice commenced (Week 105) were considered terminal and the tumors observed in these animals were categorized as incidental.
L1511: Where the test for trend was statistically significant, the highest dose group was excluded and the trend test was repeated, using a one-tailed test until the test was no longer statistically significant. The significance levels were adjusted using a continuity correction where there was one degree of freedom.
L1512: In the case of sparse tables (&lt;10 total tumor bearing animals in the groups analyzed for the trend or pairwise test), the exact form of the test was used. Otherwise, the asymptotic version of the test was used.
L1513: For each given tumor type, statistical analysis was performed if the incidence in at least one dosed group was increased by at least two occurrences over the vehicle control group. Animals were assigned to the terminal sacrifice strata based on the death or sacrifice status recorded in the data and were not assigned based on the day/week of necropsy.
L1514: ## Adjustment for the multiplicity:
L1515: Unadjusted P-values were reported for tumors. Indications of a possible treatment effect were assessed on the basis of rare or common tumor type, in line with the current FDA guidelines (Food and Drug Administration, 2001). briefly, as to trend analysis, common and rare tumors were tested at 0.005 and 0.025 significance levels, respectively. As to pairwise comparisons, common and rare tumors were tested at 0.01 and 0.05 significance levels, respectively. Site-specific background historical control database was used to determine whether the tumors should be designated as rare or common.
L1516: ## Sponsor's findings:
L1517: The sponsor's report showed that no significance was attained at the pairwise or trend tests for both male and female rats.
L1518: ## 2.2 Reviewer's analyses
L1519: To verify sponsor's analysis and to perform additional analyses suggested by the reviewing pharmacologist, this reviewer independently performed the survival and tumor data analyses. Data used in this reviewer's analyses were provided by the sponsor electronically on September 23, 2022, via SN0001.
L1520: ## 2.2.1 Survival analysis
L1521: In the reviewer's analysis, intercurrent mortality data were analyzed using the Kaplan-Meier product limit method. The Kaplan-Meier's curves were presented graphically for male and female rats separately. The dose response relationship and homogeneity of survival distributions were tested for the treatment groups using the Likelihood Ratio test and the Log-Rank test. The intercurrent mortality data are given in Tables 1A and 1B in the appendix for male and female rats, respectively. The Kaplan-Meier curves for survival rate are given in Figures 1A and 1B in the appendix for male and female rats, respectively. Results of the tests for dose response relationship and homogeneity of survivals, are given in Tables 2A and 2B in the appendix for male and female rats, respectively.
L1522: ## Reviewer's findings:
L1523: This reviewer's analysis showed the numbers of rats surviving to their terminal necropsy were 35 (48.6%), 38 (52.8%), 35 (48.6%), and 35 (48.6%) in the vehicle control, low, medium, and high dose groups, in male rats, respectively, and 20 (27.8%), 31 (43.1%), 18 (25%), and 30 (41.7%) in female rats, respectively. This reviewer's analysis showed no statistically significant increase or decrease in mortality across the vehicle control group and the three treated groups in either sex of rats. The pairwise comparisons showed no statistically significant increase or decrease in mortality between the treated groups and the vehicle control group in either sex of rats.
L1524: ## 2.2.2. Tumor data analysis
L1525: In the reviewer's analysis, the tumor data were analyzed for dose response relationship across vehicle control group and the treated groups, as well as the pairwise comparisons of vehicle control group with each of the treated groups using the Poly-k method described in the paper of Bailer and Portier (1988) and Bieler and Williams (1993). In this method, an animal that lives the full study period (w_{\max}) or dies before the terminal sacrifice with development of the tumor type being tested gets a score of s_h = 1. An animal that dies at Week w_h without development of the given tumor type before the end of the study gets a score of
L1526: s_h = \left( \frac{w_h}{w_{\max}} \right)^k &lt; 1. The adjusted group size is defined as \Sigma s_h. As an interpretation, an animal with score
L1527: s_h = 1 can be considered as a whole animal, while an animal with score s_h &lt; 1 can be considered as a partial animal. The adjusted group size \Sigma s_h is equal to N (the original group size) if all animals live up to the end of the study or if each animal develops the given tumor being tested, otherwise the adjusted group size is less than N. These adjusted group sizes are then used for the dose response relationship (or the pairwise comparison) tests using the Cochran-Armitage test. One critical point for Poly-k test is the choice of the appropriate value of k. For long term 104-week standard rat and mouse studies, a value of k=3 is suggested in the literature [Gebregziabher and Hoel (2009), Moon et al. (2003), Portier, et al. (1986)]. Hence, this reviewer used k=3 for the analysis of the data. Based on the intent to treat (ITT) principle w_{\max} was considered as 105 for both male and female rats.
L1528: For the calculation of p-values, if there were less than 10 tumor bearing animals across all treatment groups for a given tumor type, the exact tests based on the discrete permutation distribution were used, with dose levels (0, 5, 20, and 60 for male rats, and 0, 15, 45, and 135 for female rats) as scores, and asymptotic tests were used for tumor types with higher incidences. The tumor rates and the p-values of the tested tumor types are listed in Tables 3A and 3B in the appendix for male rats and female rats, respectively.
L1529: ## Multiple testing adjustments:
L1530: Following the FDA draft guidance for the carcinogenicity study design and data analysis 2001, for the two-year rat study this reviewer used significance levels of 0.005 and 0.025 for common and rare tumors, respectively in dose response relationship (trend) tests and significance levels of 0.01 and 0.05 for common and rare tumors, respectively in pairwise comparisons.
L1531: A tumor is defined as a rare tumor if the published spontaneous rate or the spontaneous rate of the vehicle control of the tumor is less than 1%, and a common tumor is defined as one with tumor rate greater than or equal to 1%.
L1532: ## Reviewer's findings:
L1533: The tumor types with p-values less than 0.05 for dose response relationship and/or pairwise comparisons of vehicle control and treated groups are reported in Table 2.

L1540: ## 3. Mouse Study
L1541: Two separate experiments were conducted, one in male mice and one in female mice. In each of these two experiments there were three treated groups, one water control group, and one vehicle control group. Three hundred and fifteen CD-1 mice of each sex were assigned randomly to one of the five groups by a stratified randomization scheme designed to achieve similar group mean body weights in equal size of 63 animals. The dose levels for the three treated groups were 83, 169, and 355 mg/kg/day for males and for females, for up to 104 weeks, as indicated in Table 3. In this review, these dose groups would be referred to as the low, medium, and high dose group, respectively. The vehicle control group received daily oral vehicle control article only [Topical Gel Placebo, 0%.], were administered in the same manner as the treated groups. The dosing of males in the high dose group (Group 5) was stopped at the end of Week 78 and the Group was terminated at the end of Week 90. Also, dosing of males in the mid dose group (Group 4) was suspended in Week 92. All other groups continued dosing until the end of the study.
L1542: For the purposes of the statistical trend analysis the weighted average dose over the 104 (Group 4) and 89 (Group 5) week study periods were calculated to be 149 and 311 mg/kg/day, respectively and these doses levels were used in the analysis.
L1543: Table 3: Experimental Design in Mouse Study
L1544: Group Name	Group No.	Target/ Actual Dose
(mg/kg/day)	Number of Animal
Male	Female	Males	Females
Water Control	1	0	0	63	63
Vehicle Control	2	0	0	63	63
Low	3	100/83	100/83	63	63
Medium	4	200/169	200/169	63	63
High	5	400/355	400/355	63	63
L1545: Group 5 dosing stopped at week 78 and terminated early at week 90.
L1546: Group 4 dosing stopped at week 92.
L1547: During the study period all animals were observed for general health/mortality and moribundity twice daily (near the start and end of each working day), abnormal findings were recorded throughout the study. Cage side observations were conducted for each carcinogenicity animal once daily during the dosing phase, except on days when detailed observations were conducted. Detailed observations were conducted for each carcinogenicity animal at least once prior to dosing on Day 1, and weekly thereafter throughout the dosing phase. Detailed physical examinations which included palpation masses were done weekly, Particular attention was paid to any superficial or palpable swellings, for which the location, size, consistency, time of first observation and subsequent history were recorded. Any animal showing signs of severe debility or intoxication, and if determined to be moribund or suffering excessively will be euthanized. Observations will include, but will not be limited to, evaluation for reaction to treatment. Histopathological examinations were performed on all animals found dead or killed moribund or sacrificed at the end of the experiment. Body weights were recorded one week before treatment commenced, on the day that treatment commenced (Week 0), at weekly intervals for the first 16 weeks of treatment, thereafter once every four weeks and before necropsy.

L1553: For male mice, the sponsor's report showed, the trend test, (Groups 2, 3, 4 and 5 included), was statistically significant (p&lt;0.001). Upon exclusion of the 355 mg/kg/day treated group (Group 5), the trend test was still significant (p&lt;0.001). Upon further exclusion of the 169 mg/kg/day treated group (Group 4), the trend test was still significant (p&lt;0.001). The pairwise comparisons of the vehicle control group with the 83, 169 and 355 mg/kg/day treated groups were statistically significant (p=0.001, p&lt;0.001 and p&lt;0.001 respectively). The pairwise comparison of the water control group with the vehicle control group was not statistically significant (p=0.472).
L1554: For female mice, the sponsor's report showed, the trend test (Groups 2, 3, 4 and 5 included), was statistically significant (p=0.005). Upon exclusion of the 355 mg/kg/day treated group (Group 5), the trend test was no longer significant (p=0.391). The pairwise comparisons of the vehicle control group with the 355 mg/kg/day treated group was statistically significant (p=0.004). None of the other pairwise comparisons were statistically significant

L1577: ## 4. Summary
L1578: In this submission, the sponsor included reports of two animal carcinogenicity studies, one in rats and one in mice. These studies were intended to assess the carcinogenic potential of BBI-4000 when administered by subcutaneous injection in rats and by dermal in mice at appropriate drug levels for about 104 weeks.
L1579: ## Rat Study:
L1580: In this study two separate experiments were conducted, one in male rats and one in female rats. In each of these two experiments there were three treated groups, and one vehicle control group. Two hundred eighty-eight Sprague-Dawley rats of each sex were assigned to three treated groups, and one vehicle control group by a stratified randomization scheme designed to achieve similar group mean body weights in equal size of 72 animals, as indicated in Table 1. The dose levels for the three treated groups were 0.3, 0.75, and 1.5 mg/kg/day for males and 0.5, 1.5, and 5 mg/kg/day for females, for up to 104 weeks. In this review, these dose groups were referred to as the low, medium, and high dose group, respectively. The vehicle control group received the vehicle (Sterile 0.9 % Sodium Chloride for Injection), administered by subcutaneous injection for about 104 weeks in the same manner as the treated groups.
L1581: This reviewer's analysis showed the numbers of rats surviving to their terminal necropsy were 35 (48.6%), 38 (52.8%), 35 (48.6%), and 35 (48.6%) in the vehicle control, low, medium, and high dose groups, in male rats, respectively, and 20 (27.8%), 31 (43.1%), 18 (25%), and 30 (41.7%) in female rats, respectively. This reviewer's analysis showed no statistically significant increase or decrease in mortality across the vehicle control group and the three treated groups in either sex of rats. The pairwise comparisons showed no statistically significant increase or decrease in mortality between the treated groups and the vehicle control group in either sex of rats.
L1582: For tumor data, this reviewer's analysis showed no tumor types with a statistically significant dose response relationship in tumor incidences with increased BBI-4000 dose in either sex of rats. The pairwise comparisons also showed no tumor types with a statistically significant increase in tumor incidences in BBI-4000 treated groups, when compared to the vehicle control group in either male or female rats.
L1583: ## **Mouse Study:**
L1584: Two separate experiments were conducted, one in male mice and one in female mice. In each of these two experiments there were three treated groups, one water control group, and one vehicle control group. Three hundred and fifteen CD-1 mice of each sex were assigned randomly to one of the five groups by a stratified randomization scheme designed to achieve similar group mean body weights in equal size of 63 animals. The dose levels for the three treated groups were 83, 169, and 355 mg/kg/day for males and for females, for up to 104 weeks, as indicated in Table 3. In this review, these dose groups would be referred to as the low, medium, and high dose group, respectively. The vehicle control group received daily oral vehicle control article only [Topical Gel Placebo, 0%], were administered in the same manner as the treated groups. The dosing of males in the high dose group (Group 5) was stopped at the end of Week 78 and the Group was terminated at the end of Week 90. Also, dosing of males in the mid dose group (Group 4) was suspended in Week 92. All other groups continued dosing until the end of the study.
L1585: For the purposes of the statistical trend analysis the weighted average dose over the 104 (Group 4) and 89 (Group 5) week study periods were calculated to be 149 and 311 mg/kg/day, respectively and these doses levels were used in the analysis.
L1586: This reviewer's analysis showed the numbers of mice surviving to their terminal necropsy were 44 (70%), 39 (62%), 21(33%), 17(27%), and 15(24%), in water control, vehicle control, low, medium, and high dose groups in male mice, respectively, and 26 (41%), 31(49%), 26(41%), 32(51%), and 16 (25%), in female mice, respectively. This reviewer's analysis showed statistically significant increase in mortality across the vehicle control group and the three treated groups in both sex of mice (p &lt; 0.0001, =0.0058, respectively). The pairwise comparisons showed statistically significant increase in mortality between all the treated groups (low, medium, and high), and the vehicle control group in male mice (p = 0.0008, &lt;0.0001, and &lt;0.0001, respectively). Also, the pairwise comparisons showed statistically significant increase in mortality between the high dose group, and the vehicle control group in female mice (p &lt; 0.0031).
L1587: For tumor data, this reviewer's analysis showed no tumor types with a statistically significant dose response relationship in tumor incidences with increased BBI-4000 dose in either sex of mice. The pairwise comparisons also showed no tumor types with a statistically significant increase in tumor incidences in BBI-4000 treated groups, when compared to the vehicle control group in either male or female mice.

The Y-axis is logarithmic, ranging from 10^{-4} to 10^{0}. The X-axis ranges from 0 to 60 hours. Both plots show a red line representing the median concentration over time, surrounded by numerous black data points representing individual subject concentrations.
In the Hyperhidrosis group, the median concentration decreases rapidly from approximately 10^{-1} ng/mL/mg at 0 hours to approximately 10^{-3} ng/mL/mg by 40 hours.
In the Healthy volunteer group, the median concentration peaks around 20 hours at approximately 10^{-2} ng/mL/mg, then declines, reaching a trough around 40 hours, and subsequently increases again towards 60 hours.
L1666: Figure 6.3.2.2: Arithmetic Mean Plasma Sofipironium Concentrations-time Profiles. The graph plots Plasma Sofipironium Concentration (ng/mL) on the Y-axis (0.0 to 1.2) against Time (Hours) on the X-axis (0 to 52). Two treatments are compared: Treatment A (Sofipironium Alone, solid line with squares) and Treatment B (Paroxetine HCl + Sofipironium, dashed line with circles). Treatment B shows significantly higher peak concentrations and total systemic exposure compared to Treatment A.
L1667: Figure 6.3.2.3: Arithmetic Mean Plasma Sofpironium Concentrations-Time Profiles. The graph plots Plasma Sofpironium Concentration (ng/mL) on the Y-axis (0 to 6) against Time (Hours) on the X-axis (0 to 52). Three treatments are compared: Treatment C (Sofpironium Alone, solid line with circles), Treatment D (Cimetidine + Sofpironium, dashed line with triangles), and Treatment E (Itraconazole + Sofpironium, dotted line with diamonds). Treatment C shows the highest peak concentration (around 5.5 ng/mL) at 2 hours, followed by a rapid decline. Treatments D and E show significantly lower peak concentrations (around 1.5 ng/mL) and slower declines, indicating reduced systemic exposure when coadministered with Cimetidine or Itraconazole.
L1668: Line graph showing Placebo-Adjusted Mean Change from Baseline-QTcF Interval (\Delta\Delta\text{QTcF}) across time points (0.5h to 24h) for three treatments. The Y-axis is \Delta\Delta\text{QTcF} \pm 90\% \text{CI} (msec), ranging from -9 to 18. The X-axis is Time (h). The legend identifies three treatments: Sofipironium bromide 15% 173 mg (black line), Sofipironium (Occluded) 1038 mg (yellow line), and Moxifloxacin 400 mg (blue line). The Moxifloxacin group shows a significant increase in \Delta\Delta\text{QTcF} peaking around 15 msec at 1.0h, while the Sofipironium groups show minimal change, generally remaining close to 0 msec.

L1673: Figure 5 displays two box plots illustrating Gravimetric Sweat Production (GSP) data from Study 302 (Observed Cases).
The left plot shows GSP (Y-axis, 0 to 2500) for four groups: Veh-BL (Vehicle Baseline), SB-BL (Sofipironium Bromide Gel Baseline), Veh-EOT (Vehicle End of Treatment), and SB-EOT (Sofipironium Bromide Gel End of Treatment). The median GSP values are labeled: Veh-BL (231), SB-BL (208), Veh-EOT (91), and SB-EOT (70).
The right plot shows Change in GSP (Y-axis, -1000 to 1000) for two groups: Veh (Vehicle) and SB (Sofipironium Bromide Gel). The median Change in GSP values are labeled: Veh (-132) and SB (-146).
L1674: Figure 6 displays two line graphs comparing the Median Change from Baseline to End of Treatment in Gravimetric Sweat Production (Y-axis) versus Study Day (X-axis) for Studies 301 and 302. The X-axis for both studies ranges from 1 to 43 days. The Y-axis ranges from 0 to -150. The legend indicates two groups: SB Gel, 15% (solid line with circles) and Vehicle (dashed line with triangles).
Study 301: Both groups show a rapid decrease in median change from Day 1 (0) to Day 8 (SB Gel: approximately -100, Vehicle: approximately -100). The SB Gel group continues to decrease, reaching approximately -130 at Day 43. The Vehicle group stabilizes around -100 after Day 8.
Study 302: Both groups show a rapid decrease in median change from Day 1 (0) to Day 8 (SB Gel: approximately -100, Vehicle: approximately -100). The SB Gel group continues to decrease, reaching approximately -140 at Day 43. The Vehicle group stabilizes around -100 after Day 8.
L1675: Figure 7 displays two line graphs comparing the Median Value of Gravimetric Sweat Production (Y-axis) versus Study Day (X-axis) for Studies 301 and 302. The X-axis for both studies ranges from 1 to 43 days. The Y-axis ranges from 0 to 250. The legend indicates two groups: SB Gel, 15% (solid line with circles) and Vehicle (dashed line with triangles).
Study 301: Both groups start at a high median value (SB Gel: approximately 210, Vehicle: approximately 230) on Day 1. Both groups decrease significantly by Day 8 (SB Gel: approximately 120, Vehicle: approximately 150). The SB Gel group continues to decrease, stabilizing around 70-80 after Day 36. The Vehicle group stabilizes around 100-120 after Day 36.
Study 302: Both groups start at a high median value (SB Gel: approximately 210, Vehicle: approximately 230) on Day 1. Both groups decrease significantly by Day 8 (SB Gel: approximately 90, Vehicle: approximately 150). The SB Gel group continues to decrease, stabilizing around 70-80 after Day 36. The Vehicle group stabilizes around 100-120 after Day 36.
L1676: Scatter plot showing Median Change in Gravimetric Sweat Production (Y-axis, ranging from -300 to 0) versus Analysis Center (X-axis, numbered 1 to 17). The plot compares SB Gel, 15% (circles) and Vehicle (triangles). The legend indicates SB Gel, 15% (circles) and Vehicle (triangles). The plot shows significant variability in median change across analysis centers. A dashed horizontal line is present around -100, and a solid horizontal line is present around -150. The numbers above the X-axis labels (e.g., 32, 26, 25, 23, 24, 19, 21, 13, 14, 15, 13, 14, 14, 14, 16) represent the total number of subjects per analysis center.
L1677: Figure 9 is a scatter plot showing the Median Change in Gravimetric Sweat Production (Y-axis, ranging from -500 to -100) versus Analysis Center (X-axis, numbered 1 to 15). The plot compares SB Gel 15% (represented by circles) and Vehicle (represented by triangles). The legend indicates SB Gel 15% and Vehicle. The Y-axis is labeled 'Median' and the X-axis is labeled 'Analysis Center'. The numbers below the X-axis represent the total number of subjects per analysis center. The median change for SB Gel 15% is generally lower (more negative) than for Vehicle across most analysis centers, with a large outlier at Analysis Center 12 for SB Gel 15% (around -450).
L1678: Figure 10 displays four line graphs showing HDSM-Ax-7 Scale Scores (Y-axis, 0 to 4) over Date (X-axis) for subjects with multiple enrollments in Studies 301 or 302. The graphs are labeled (b) (6) in the top right corner of each plot area.
The plots illustrate the trajectory of HDSM-Ax-7 scores across Study Weeks (0 through 6, where 6 may represent Week 6 or EOT). Blue solid lines represent randomizations to SB gel, and gray dashed lines represent randomizations to vehicle.
Subjects are classified based on the final score (Week 6 or EOT): Y (responder, \ge 2 point improvement) or N (non-responder, \ge 2 point improvement). Enrollments without a Y or N noted were handled as missing data.
The four plots show different subjects and time periods:
Top Left: Shows scores from January to March. Subject 6 (Y) shows a low score (around 0.5) at Week 6.
Top Right: Shows scores from March 15 to April 5. Subject 6 (N) shows a score around 1.5 at Week 6.
Bottom Left: Shows scores from March 1 to April 15. Subject 6 (N) shows a score around 2.5 at Week 6.
Bottom Right: Shows scores from February 1 to March 15. Subject 6 (Y) shows a score around 1.5 at Week 6.
L1679: Line graph showing Body Weight (g) versus Week for male mice in the Dermal Mouse Carcinogenicity Study. The X-axis represents Week (from PD to 104), and the Y-axis represents Body weight (g) (from 0.0 to 60.0). Five groups are plotted: 1M (Untreated water control males, solid line), 2M (Vehicle control males, dashed line), 3M (5% sofipironium bromide gel males, dotted line), 4M (10% sofipironium bromide gel males, dash-dot line), and 5M (20% sofipironium bromide gel males, long dashed line). All groups show a rapid increase in body weight during the initial weeks (up to Week 32), followed by a plateau or slight increase. The 5M group generally maintains the highest body weight, while the 3M group shows the lowest body weight, especially after Week 32.
L1680: Line graph showing Body Weight (g) versus Week for Female Mice in the Dermal Mouse Carcinogenicity Study. The X-axis represents Week (P0 to 104), and the Y-axis represents Body weight (g) (0.0 to 40.0). Five groups are plotted: 1F (Untreated water control females, solid line), 2F (Vehicle control females, dashed line), 3F (5% sofipironium bromide gel females, dotted line), 4F (10% sofipironium bromide gel females, dash-dot line), and 5F (20% sofipironium bromide gel females, long dashed line). All groups show a similar growth curve, starting around 20g at P0 and stabilizing between 30g and 35g by Week 104.
